| [M24](linux.md#m24) | linux | amdgpu's init on unit A, ordered: no host write to any microcode port (firmware goes in through 11 PSP ring commands in 35 ms), about 900 non-display writes in the order GART, IH, PSP, SMU tables, golden registers, RLC, un-halt CP/MEC, KIQ by MMIO, SDMA, interrupts; two thirds of all writes are display-core AUX traffic. Corrected the same day: the 99 844 polls of `MMVM_INVALIDATE_ENG17_SEM` at the first MMHUB TLB flush (0.21 s, dmesg timeout) are **an artifact of our own pre-driver sweep**, not amdgpu's normal init on this unit, see M25 |
| [M25](hardware.md#m25) | hardware | Reading is not side-effect free: a read of any `GCVM_INVALIDATE_ENGn_SEM` / `MMVM_INVALIDATE_ENGn_SEM` **acquires** that invalidation semaphore (first read 1, every later read 0 until somebody writes 0), `CP_*_HEADER_DUMP` registers advance on each read, and a read of `GRBM_GFX_CNTL` latches `GRBM_READ_ERROR` (`0x80008088`: error bit plus that register's address) |
| [M26](kmd.md#m26) | kmd | A display-only WDDM driver can own `1002:13FE` on unit A and live on the firmware's framebuffer: install without reboot, 1920x1200 kept, no GC register changed by the switch, survives disable/enable and a restart, uninstall returns the device to Basic Display |
| [M27](tooling.md#m27) | tooling | Lab facts for the Windows side: KDNET works over the on-board RTL8168 (`busparams 4.0.0`, the OS then uses the debugger's virtual adapter); safe mode with networking has network and SSH over that wired path but not over the USB Wi-Fi dongle (vendor driver not loaded there); with a debugger attached the ACPI interpreter reports AML errors `0x3a` once and `0x78` several times at every boot (firmware tables, not investigated); one warm restart ended in a black screen before the kernel started and needed the reset button, four others went through |
| [M28](kmd.md#m28) | kmd | Our own miniport `bc250kmd` (display-only, no MMIO) owns `1002:13FE` on unit A: install without reboot, 1920x1200 on the firmware's framebuffer, breadcrumbs reach stage 61 in order, no GC register outside the noise set changes, survives disable/enable and a restart (start budget 1 -> 2 -> confirmed to 0 by `bc250mon`), rollback to Basic Display and re-install both work. Milestone M3 reached |
| [M29](kmd.md#m29) | kmd | `D3DKMTEscape` with `D3DKMT_ESCAPE_DRIVERPRIVATE` **does reach `DxgkDdiEscape` of a display-only driver**: an elevated user-mode tool gets `STATUS_SUCCESS` and the driver's data back (`BC250_ESCAPE_GET_INFO`). Without an escape handler of ours the same call returns `STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER` (Basic Display) or `STATUS_NOT_SUPPORTED` (KMDOD sample). `D3DKMTOpenAdapterFromDeviceName` accepts only interface paths of `GUID_DISPLAY_DEVICE_ARRIVAL` |
| [M30](hardware.md#m30) | hardware | Register writes work under Windows through our miniport, at regcalc's addresses: `SCRATCH_REG0` (`0x30100`) and `SCRATCH_REG1` (`0x30104`) took `0xCAFEDEAD` / `0x0BC25001` written through a gated escape of `bc250kmd`, an independent driver (`bc250rd`, own mapping) read the same values back, and a full GC sweep shows no other register changed. Reads through both drivers agree bit for bit. With the gates closed, or for offsets outside the generated tables, the driver refuses |
| [M31](hardware.md#m31) | hardware | Under Windows the VRAM carve-out is reachable from the CPU by system physical address, exactly as amdgpu reaches it: `GCMC_VM_FB_OFFSET << 24` = `0x270000000`, length 8 GB from `GCMC_VM_FB_LOCATION_BASE/TOP`, MC base `0xF400000000`. The range is outside every range of `MmGetPhysicalMemoryRanges` (Linux's e820 map of the same unit marks it reserved). BAR0 (`0xC0000000`, where the firmware framebuffer is) shows the same memory from VRAM offset 0: framebuffer pixels and a test page read identically through both paths, words written through one path read back through the other. The top 2 MB, where amdgpu keeps its GART table on this unit, read without fault. All 147 VM/MC location, aperture, context 0 and L2 registers of GC and MMHUB read the same under Windows as in the firmware state amdgpu started from |
| [M32](hardware.md#m32) | hardware | BAR0 is not a coherent view of VRAM: after a word was written through BAR0 and then changed through the physical address, BAR0 kept returning its own last value for at least 45 s (both mappings uncached on the CPU side), and agreed again a few reads later. The BAR0 path goes through HDP, which amdgpu flushes explicitly. The driver therefore accesses VRAM by physical address only |
| [M33](hardware.md#m33) | hardware | GART and VM context 0 come up under Windows with AMD's own code: `gfxhub_v2_0.c` and `mmhub_v2_0.c`, imported unmodified from mainline v6.18 and run inside `bc250kmd` against our shim, issue the same 285 register writes in the same order as amdgpu did on this unit (values equal except four registers that carry the addresses of our scratch and dummy pages); both engine-17 TLB invalidations are acknowledged, the MMHUB semaphore protocol works, no protection fault status bit is set, the registers read back through an independent driver as written (two self-clearing, as under Linux), the hardware's own responses (`SDMA0/1_UTCL1_INV0`, request/acknowledge) equal Linux's state after init, and the firmware's scan-out keeps running. Page table: top 2 MB of VRAM, MC `0xF5FFE00000`, where amdgpu puts it. The firmware state can be written back (282 registers); six status registers keep the record of the invalidation. On the kernel that produced the E03 trace (6.18.52) `gmc.noretry` is true for GC 10.1.3, on mainline v6.18 it would be false; the driver uses the measured value |
| [M34](hardware.md#m34) | hardware | The PSP takes the GPU firmware under Windows, through AMD's own `psp_v11_0_8.c` (imported unmodified) and amdgpu's command sequence run inside `bc250kmd`: ring create, `SETUP_TMR` (4 MB at MC `0xF5FF800000`) and ten `LOAD_IP_FW` (SDMA0, SDMA1, CE, PFP, ME, MEC1, MEC1 jump table, MEC2, MEC2 jump table, RLC), every fence returned with status 0, the 15 register writes equal to amdgpu's on this unit in offset, order and value, command times equal to amdgpu's spacing (0.13 ms jump tables, 1.9 ms SDMA, 5.9 ms CP). Images staged in VRAM, not behind the GART. The response carries a TMR address for the CP images only (CE `+0x42000`, PFP `+0x83000`, ME `+0xC4000`, MEC1 and MEC2 `+0`); for SDMA, jump tables and RLC it is 0. `DESTROY_TMR` and ring stop are accepted (`C2PMSG_64 = 0x80030000` afterwards) and a second ring, TMR and load are accepted in the same boot |
| [M35](hardware.md#m35) | hardware | Loading through the PSP is not passive: the PSP starts what it loads. After the ten `LOAD_IP_FW` and with no other register written by us, `SDMA0/1_F32_CNTL` read 0 instead of 1 (halt released, `SDMA0/1_STATUS2_REG` changes from sweep to sweep), `RLC_CNTL` reads 1 and 31 GC registers hold the value Linux shows after amdgpu's init (RLC power-gating and SERDES state, `CGTS_*_SM_CTRL_REG`, `CC_GC_SHADER_ARRAY_CONFIG_GEN0`, `RLC_SMU_COMMAND = 0x13`: the RLC has talked to the SMU). `CP_ME_CNTL` (`0x15000000`) and `CP_MEC_CNTL` (`0x50000000`) do not change: the CP stays halted until the driver releases it. An unload (`DESTROY_TMR`, ring stop) does not undo this; a warm reboot does. A second load in the same boot meets the running RLC and leaves it disabled and busy (`RLC_CNTL = 0`, `RLC_STAT = 5`, `GRBM_STATUS2` bit 24), the former claim that amdgpu avoids this by stopping RLC is unproven: the generic SMU cleanup has a conditional stop callback, but E13 reload also failed and its unload traces lack that write (M333) |
| [M36](hardware.md#m36) | hardware | The GPU executes commands under Windows: amdgpu's `gfx_v10_0_hw_init()` and `sdma_v5_0_hw_init()` for this part, transcribed against AMD's unmodified tables and structures (`driver/shim/bc250_gfx.c`, `bc250_sdma.c`) and run inside `bc250kmd` after GART enable (M33) and the PSP load (M34), bring up RLC, KIQ, MEC, eight compute queues and the gfx queue, and all eleven ring tests pass: a `SET_UCONFIG_REG` packet written by the CPU into each ring changes `SCRATCH_REG0` from `0xCAFEDEAD` to `0xDEADBEEF` (milestone M5) |
| [M37](kmd.md#m37) | kmd | GPU access to system memory through the GART works under Windows with the CPU physical address as bus address: rings, write-back slots and EOP buffers are contiguous non-paged system memory entered into the GART table of M33 with AMD's PTE format (`0x0003000000000077` flags: valid, system, snooped, read, write, execute, MTYPE UC), and the CP fetches and executes from them |
| [M38](kmd.md#m38) | kmd | Windows gives a display-only WDDM miniport a message-signalled interrupt for this function: with the documented `Interrupt Management\MessageSignaledInterruptProperties` values (`MSISupported = 1`, `MessageNumberLimit = 1`) in the INF's `.HW` section the translated interrupt resource is a message (`CM_RESOURCE_INTERRUPT_MESSAGE`) from the first device start after the install, no reboot needed (0.5.x without the section: line interrupt, IRQ 52). With no interrupt source enabled the interrupt routine is never called: not over register sweeps, presents, device restarts, nor while RLC, CP and SDMA run and execute ring tests with the IH ring off |
| [M39](hardware.md#m39) | hardware | A second GFX bring-up in the same boot works after the undo of M37, with one declared deviation from amdgpu: where `gfx_v10_0_kiq_init_register()` finds the KIQ's HQD still active and the MEC halted, the driver writes `CP_HQD_ACTIVE = 0` instead of a dequeue request that no running engine could answer (`driver/amdgpu-import/PROVENANCE.md`; upstream's only such write is SR-IOV's, `gfx_v10_0.c:7029`) |
| [M40](linux.md#m40) | linux | Linux reference for the interrupt path on unit A (amdgpu, kernel 6.18.52-0-lts): amdgpu runs this function on **MSI-X** (capability `Enable+ Count=3`, MSI `Enable-`), one vector in use. amdgpu's own IB tests (`amdgpu_test_ib`, one fence per ring) cost exactly 12 interrupts and 12 IH vectors (`IH_RB_RPTR = IH_RB_WPTR` advance by `0x180` = 12 x 32 bytes), in three boots out of three. The vectors: gfx end of pipe `client 20, source 181, ring id 0`; the eight compute queues `client 20, source 181`, ring ids 4, 5, 6, 7, 20, 21, 22, 23; the KIQ `client 20, source 178, ring id 9`; SDMA0 and SDMA1 traps `client 8` and `client 9`, `source 224`. Idle state after init: `IH_RB_CNTL = 0x403301A1`, `IH_CNTL = 0x01000000`, `IH_DOORBELL_RPTR = 0x100002F0`, `BIF_IH_DOORBELL_RANGE = 0x00020BC0`, `CP_INT_CNTL_RING0 = 0x05C00000`, `CP_ME1_PIPE0..3_INT_CNTL = 0x05800000`, `CP_ME2_PIPE1_INT_CNTL = 0x20000000` (the KIQ's pipe), `CP_ME2_PIPE0_INT_CNTL = 0`. `CPC_INT_CNTL` read under an SRBM selection is the selected pipe's `CP_MEn_PIPEm_INT_CNTL` (me 2 pipe 0 reads 0, me 2 pipe 1 reads `0x20000000`, me 1 reads `0x05800000`). Every active queue idles with `CP_HQD_PQ_RPTR = CP_HQD_PQ_WPTR_LO`; compute queue 0 has pipe priority 2 and queue priority 15, the other seven 0 and 0. A thirteenth active HQD, me 2 pipe 0 queue 0 (2 KB ring, doorbell offset `0x3000`, MQD and ring in GART space), is amdkfd's HIQ, not part of `gfx_v10_0_hw_init()` |
| [M41](linux.md#m41) | linux | amdgpu's init on unit A is reproducible to the register write: a clean trace (stick in mode `readonly`: a short list of named reads, no write, no sweep, then `modprobe amdgpu` with tracefs armed) holds all 10018 register writes of the E03 trace in the same order at the same offsets. 7 of them differ in value: three carry the dummy page's address (`GCVM_` and `MMVM_L2_PROTECTION_FAULT_DEFAULT_ADDR_LO32`, `INTERRUPT_CNTL2`), four are writes to one register at dword `0x5DF5` that none of our steps touches. The clean trace has 478 more writes, all 2.2 s after the last common one (the console's mode set, outside E03's window), and 37861 reads against E03's 136117. "Timeout waiting for sem acquire in VM flush" (M24) appears in none of three boots without our sweep: M25's attribution of it to the sweep stands |
| [M42](linux.md#m42) | linux | `modprobe -r amdgpu` hung unit A under Linux (console unbound, the stick's QR viewer stopped, tracefs armed; kernel 6.18.52-0-lts, amdkfd active): the machine stopped answering on the network within seconds and needed the power button. Cause unknown, the trace was in RAM and is lost |
| [M43](hardware.md#m43) | hardware | The GPU interrupts the CPU under Windows, through a display-only miniport: amdgpu's `navi10_ih_irq_init()` transcribed (`driver/shim/bc250_ih.c`) and run inside `bc250kmd` programs the IH ring with the same 15 register writes as amdgpu on this unit except three address registers; with the ring on and no source enabled nothing arrives. After amdgpu's 35 interrupt-source writes (equal in offset, order and value) a fence with the interrupt bit on the gfx ring gives exactly one interrupt routine call, one DPC (`DxgkCbQueueDpc` works for a display-only miniport) and one IH vector, `client 20, source 181, ring id 0, src_data[0] = 0x80000000`: the vector Linux gets on the same machine (M40). Compute queues answer with their ring ids (pipe 0 queue 0: 4, pipe 1 queue 1: 21, pipe 3 queue 0: 7), the KIQ with `source 178, ring id 9`. The same fence without the interrupt bit delivers its value and no interrupt. A hundred fences in one call: 100 values, 100 vectors, 1082 us, none lost, no overflow (102 interrupts for 104 vectors: MSI messages coalesce, the DPC takes what is there) |
| [M44](hardware.md#m44) | hardware | The MEC keeps a queue's fetch state across a halt, outside the `CP_HQD_*` registers: a second bring-up in one device start, with every buffer at a new GART address (the IH ring held its pages, so the allocator handed out others), fails at the KIQ ring test, and the GPU reports a VMID 0 read fault at page `0x444000`, which is the FIRST run's KIQ ring (base `0x442000`) plus `0x2000` bytes, about where that ring's read pointer stood. No register write of either run names that address. The fault arrives through our own IH ring (`client 27` UTCL2, `source 0`, `src_data 0x444, 0x50`) and the witness reads `GCVM_L2_PROTECTION_FAULT_STATUS = 0x8B0`, `ADDR_LO32 = 0x444`. This is what M39 really measured: there the second ring landed on the first one's address, the stale fetch walked valid NOPs (hence `CP_HQD_PQ_RPTR = 0x737` and 5 ms), and writing `CP_HQD_ACTIVE = 0` under halt (the declared deviation) does not take the queue down. `mqd->cp_hqd_pq_rptr = 0`, added after M39, treated the symptom; its prediction (stage 6 back to about 350 us) was never reached because the run fails first |
| [M45](hardware.md#m45) | hardware | During the undo, unmapping the eight compute queues through the KIQ raises eight `source 181` vectors with `src_data[0] = 1` (a fence's end-of-pipe carries 0, the gfx ring's `0x80000000`); their ring ids were 20, 21, 22, 7, 20, 21, 22, 7, not the eight queues' own ids: the queue field seems to name the queue last active on the pipe |
| [M46](linux.md#m46) | linux | What amdgpu tells a user-mode driver about unit A (every `AMDGPU_INFO` query Mesa makes, raw, kernel 6.18.52-0-lts): `DEV_INFO` is 93 words with device `0x13fe`, chip revision 2, external revision `0x84`, family `0x8f`; `HW_IP_INFO` reports GFX version 10.0 (IP discovery version `0x000a0103`, 10.1.3) with one ring, COMPUTE the same with `available_rings = 0xf` (four of the eight kernel compute rings are offered to user mode), DMA version 5.0 (`0x00050001`) with two rings (`0x3`), IB start alignment 32 for GFX and COMPUTE and 256 for DMA; no UVD, VCE or VCN block answers; `AMDGPU_INFO_UQ_FW_AREAS` fails with 95 (no user queues); the registers Mesa derives its configuration from read `GB_ADDR_CONFIG = 0x00000044`, `CC_GC_SHADER_ARRAY_CONFIG = 0xfff80000`; amdkfd lists the GPU as node 1 with `gfx_target_version 100103`, `simd_count 48`, 2 SDMA engines, 24 CP queues, `max_engine_clk_fcompute 2000` |
| [M47](hardware.md#m47) | hardware | Platform facts read under Linux on unit A (BIOS P3.00 12/09/2021): the firmware publishes **no IVRS table** (ACPI tables present: APIC BGRT CDIT CRAT DSDT FACP FACS FIDT FPDT HPET MCFG SSDT1 SSDT2 UEFI VFCT) and the kernel logs no AMD-Vi unit, so there is no IOMMU between the GPU and memory: a bus address is a physical address; VFCT (55428 bytes) carries the video BIOS `113-AMDRBN-003`; the GPU function reports a 16.0 GT/s x16 link; the power tables offer `sclk` 1000/1500/2000 MHz with 1500 MHz active at idle, `mclk` and `fclk` 450 MHz (the sysfs string only: on M789's reading that number is not the memory data rate), overdrive range 1000 to 2000 MHz and 700 to 1129 mV, 906 mV at 1500 MHz; at idle on the stick's console hwmon read 63 W PPT, 70 C edge, `vddnb` 1187 mV; `gpu_busy_percent` is not supported; twelve `\_PR.C00x` lookups fail while the SSDTs load (the AML errors seen before) |

| [M49](linux.md#m49) | linux | libdrm's gfx10 memset compute shader (`shader_code_gfx10.h`, 9 dwords) and its 72-dword PM4 program run correctly on unit A's gfx1013 under amdgpu: submitted through raw ioctls on `AMDGPU_HW_IP_COMPUTE`, 1 workgroup fills 256 of 256 dwords with `0x22222222`, 16 workgroups 4096 of 4096, with and without `AMDGPU_IB_FLAG_EMIT_MEM_SYNC` (amdgpu does not emit `ACQUIRE_MEM` for a user compute IB unless that flag is set, and libdrm's test does not set it). | [M50](linux.md#m50) | linux | Mesa's RADV drives unit A under Linux as `AMD BC-250 (RADV GFX1013)`, Vulkan 1.4 (Mesa 26.1.6, drm 3.64.0), and computes correctly: eight compute tests with exactly defined results (constant fill, integer hash, saxpy, naive and tiled sgemm, a two-layer perceptron, a reduction) give GPU hashes equal to the CPU's in every run, a deliberately wrong shader is caught, `sgemm` and `sgemm_tiled` agree bit for bit. As Mesa sees the part: name `GFX1013`, IP GFX 10.1 with one queue and SDMA 5.0 with two and no COMPUTE IP at all, 2 SE, 16 RB, 24 CU, 16 TCC blocks, `GB_ADDR_CONFIG 0x00100044` (amdgpu's golden constant, not the register's `0x00000044` of M46), `pa_sc_tile_steering_override 0x122000`, PCIe gen 4 x16, firmware feature 32. | [M51](linux.md#m51) | linux | llama.cpp (build 9564) on its Vulkan backend runs on unit A under Linux and is deterministic: greedy text from stories15M Q4_0 and TinyLlama 1.1B Q4_0 is identical in 4 of 4 GPU runs each (sha256 `9a75289c5ee188d7`, `8a5491652c871b51`); the GPU's text equals the CPU's for the two small models and parts from it at about token 40 for the 1.1B model. TinyLlama 1.1B Q4_0 at 1500 MHz: 1657 t/s prompt (pp512) and 220 t/s generation (tg128) on the GPU against 111 and 57 on the CPU's six threads. | [M52](hardware.md#m52) | hardware | Unit A's GPU throughput follows its shader clock almost linearly, and amdgpu offers one way to set that clock: `power_dpm_force_performance_level low` and `high` are refused (EINVAL), `pp_od_clk_voltage` works (`vc 0 <MHz> <mV>`, `c`; `r`, `c` to undo) and always forces a voltage with the clock. | [M53](linux.md#m53) | linux | amdgpu's GPU recovery does not reset this part and hangs the machine: `amdgpu_gpu_recover` on unit A (kernel 6.18.52-0-lts) logs `MODE1 reset`, `GPU psp mode1 reset`, `GPU reset succeeded, trying to resume`, but the streamed register trace holds no reset command to any PSP mailbox register, because `psp_v11_0_8_funcs` has no `.mode1_reset` and `psp_mode1_reset()` then answers 0 (`amdgpu_psp.h:488-489`, `psp_v11_0_8.c:175-180`). The resume therefore ran on hardware that was never reset: the PSP accepted a second ring creation and all eleven load frames in the same boot, the SMU resumed, and the machine hung hard 173 ms after the first register access, the last complete write being `RLC_CNTL = 1` (RLC started again over a running system) |
| [M54](linux.md#m54) | linux | amdgpu's unload on unit A, traced (`modprobe -r amdgpu` with the console unbound, rc 0, machine alive): outside the display block it turns the CP interrupt enables off, runs the KIQ ring test that ends the queue unmap, stops SDMA (`SDMA0/1_CNTL`, ring and IB off, `F32_CNTL = 1`), halts the CP (`CP_ME_CNTL = 0x15000000`, `CP_MEC_CNTL = 0x50000000`), destroys the PSP's ring (`C2PMSG_64 = 0x00030000`), switches the IH ring, the VM contexts of both hubs and the doorbell aperture off. It writes no `CP_HQD_DEQUEUE_REQUEST`, no other HQD register and no `RLC_CNTL`: the KIQ's queue is never taken from the MEC and the RLC keeps running |
| [M55](linux.md#m55) | linux | A second `modprobe amdgpu` in the same boot, after that clean unload, hangs unit A. No register trace (with the module unloaded `:mod:amdgpu` arms no event) |
| [M56](linux.md#m56) | linux | Sleep on unit A under Linux: the firmware offers S0, S4 and S5 only, `mem_sleep` is `[s2idle]`, and the RTC has no alarm (`rtc_cmos ... no alarms`, no `wakealarm` file), so the board cannot wake itself. amdgpu enters s2idle with the warning that the BIOS is not configured for it. One resume by the board's power button after 467 s: kernel, network and amdgpu's module alive, no amdgpu message at resume, GPU function after it not tested. A second attempt did not react to the button |
| [M57](hardware.md#m57) | hardware | A compute dispatch runs under Windows on a kernel-owned MEC queue, which is M6's exit criterion: libdrm's gfx10 memset shader (`shader_test_util.c`, transcribed in `driver/shim/bc250_dispatch.c`), 64 threads per workgroup, fills a GTT buffer seeded with `0xCAFEDEAD` with `0x22222222`. 1 workgroup and 16 workgroups on compute ring 0 and 16 workgroups on ring 5 (after a second bring-up): the check reads every dword as asked and the seed untouched behind the last workgroup, 26 to 28 us from doorbell to fence, one end-of-pipe vector per dispatch (client 20, source 181, ring id 4 or 21). Unit A at 67 to 71 C |
| [M58](hardware.md#m58) | hardware | M44's defect is fixed by taking the KIQ from the MEC while the MEC still runs: with the undo of 0.6.2 (KIQ dequeue request before the halt, one step more than amdgpu's own unload, M54) a second and a third GFX bring-up in one device start pass all eight stages (stage 6, the CP with its ring tests, in 313 us), with every ring at a GART address the earlier runs never used, and no UTCL2 fault vector arrives. Fences on the gfx ring, the KIQ and a compute ring and a 16-workgroup dispatch work after the second bring-up. The write sequences differ from the first run's only in addresses and in read-modify-writes that start from our own undo's values instead of the firmware's |
| [M59](hardware.md#m59) | hardware | An SDMA engine keeps its ring pointers across a halt, and they cannot be written back to zero: after the first bring-up's submissions `SDMA0_GFX_RB_RPTR = RB_WPTR = RB_RPTR_FETCH = 0x100` (4 submissions of 16 dwords) and SDMA1's `0xC0` (3); the undo halts the engines (`F32_CNTL = 1`) and the values stay; the next bring-up writes `RB_RPTR`, `RB_WPTR` and their high halves to 0 and `RB_WPTR = 0` again under `MINOR_PTR_UPDATE = 1`, exactly as `sdma_v5_0_gfx_resume_instance()` does, and a witness sweep taken before any submission still reads `0x100` and `0xC0`, while `RB_BASE` took the new address. A ring test that then rings the doorbell with `0x40` executes nothing and times out (slot keeps the seed, pointers unchanged): the write pointer is 64 bits wide and only ever counts up, so a doorbell below it is ignored. In the first bring-up both engines work: ring test in 3 us, three fences each, vectors client 8 and client 9, source 224, as under Linux; the ring test itself raises no vector |
| [M60](hardware.md#m60) | hardware | Adopting the engine's write pointer makes SDMA work across re-inits: with 0.7.0 (the bring-up reads `RB_WPTR` back after upstream's writes of 0 and, if the engine kept it, continues from it: `ring->wptr`, the `RB_WPTR` write under `MINOR_PTR_UPDATE` and the write-back slot) three bring-ups in one device start each pass both SDMA ring tests inside stage 7, two more ring tests, five fences and a 16-workgroup dispatch. The witness shows the pointers counting on across the undo and across three different ring bases: SDMA0 `0x40, 0x140 / 0x180, 0x280 / 0x2C0, 0x3C0`, SDMA1 `0x40, 0x100 / 0x140, 0x200 / 0x240, 0x300` (before and after each run's submissions). Every undo returned 0, so both engines halted drained. New and unexplained: one extra SDMA0 vector per re-init, `client 8 source 244` (`SDMA_DOORBELL_INVALID`) with data `0x280` before the second run's fences and `client 8 source 0` with data `0x500` before the third's; none from SDMA1, none in the first bring-up, nothing failed after them |
| [M61](kmd.md#m61) | kmd | `DxgkCbAcquirePostDisplayOwnership` hands our miniport a `DXGK_DISPLAY_INFORMATION` whose `TargetId` is `0xFFFFFFFF` (`D3DDDI_ID_UNINITIALIZED`) when the driver is loaded under a running desktop (install over a running older build): mode, pitch and format are right, the target id is not a target of ours. The id is not dxgkrnl's knowledge of our children (the callback runs inside `DxgkDdiStartDevice`, before it has asked for them); it follows what the previous owner of the display said when it released it: the two loads that read `0xFFFFFFFF` took over from 0.7.0 and 0.7.1, whose `DxgkDdiStopDeviceAndReleasePostDisplayOwnership` did not fill `DisplayInfo->TargetId`, and the two loads that took over from 0.7.2 and 0.7.3, which do fill it, read `2424833` (`0x250001`, our child UID). Whatever needs a VidPN target id (CRTC_VSYNC reports, `GetScanLine`) must use the child's UID and not this field |
| [M62](kmd.md#m62) | kmd | The first full WDDM start is refused by dxgkrnl after the start, quietly and reversibly: with `EnableFullWddm = 1` (bc250kmd 0.7.2, `DxgkInitialize` with the WDDM 2.0 table of `wddm.c`, GpuMmu declared, one node, one local segment, no user-mode driver) `DriverEntry` and `DxgkDdiStartDevice` succeed, no `CommitVidPn` ever arrives, dxgkrnl calls `DxgkDdiStopDevice` and unloads the driver, and the device shows `CM_PROB_FAILED_POST_START` with problem status 0. No TDR, no bugcheck, no live kernel report; Basic Display takes the desktop, SSH and the overlay keep working; closing the gate and re-enabling the device brings the display-only driver back to stage 61. Windows records no reason and the driver's log ring is lost with the unload, so which answer dxgkrnl refused is NOT known from this run |
| [M63](kmd.md#m63) | kmd | The refusal of M62 happens on the `DXGKQAITYPE_DRIVERCAPS` answer: between `DxgkDdiStartDevice` returning success and `DxgkDdiStopDevice`, dxgkrnl (file version 10.0.22621.6199 on unit A) enters exactly one DDI of the full table, `DxgkDdiQueryAdapterInfo` type 1 with a 576-byte output buffer (the `DXGK_DRIVERCAPS` size the driver was compiled with), gets `STATUS_SUCCESS`, and calls `DxgkDdiStopDevice` 2 ms later. No segment query, no `GPUMMUCAPS`, no `GetNodeMetadata`, no device, context or VidPN call, no `ControlInterrupt`. The answer refused: `WDDMVersion = DXGKDDI_WDDMv2`, `SchedulingCaps 0x45`, `MemoryManagementCaps 0x60` (VirtualAddressingSupported, GpuMmuSupported, paging node 0), `FlipCaps 0x2`, one node, `SupportNonVGA = 1`, and `SupportPerEngineTDR`, `SupportDirectFlip`, `SupportSmoothRotation`, `MaxAllocationListSlotId` all 0. The DxgKrnl event channels stay empty; Kernel-PnP logs only "reported to be failing". What is at fault is NOT decided by this run: it need not be a member of the answer at all, only something dxgkrnl checks between this query and the next (see M64) |
| [M64](kmd.md#m64) | kmd | The refusal of M62/M63 is about the missing user-mode driver name, not about the caps: read offline in unit A's `dxgkrnl.sys` 10.0.22621.6199, `DXGADAPTER::Initialize` fails with `STATUS_OBJECT_NAME_NOT_FOUND` ("UMD name is missing and device is not compute only") right after the DRIVERCAPS query when the driver's software key has no `UserModeDriverName`, and that is the only failing exit before the next `QueryAdapterInfo` call. A full (non compute-only) WDDM adapter cannot start without a registered UMD name on this build; the "plain package" of E16 can never pass. The same reading predicts the next refusals of the 0.7.3 table: `DxgkDdiSetStablePowerState` NULL at interface `0x5023`, and, if dxgkrnl's enforcement switch is on, `SupportDirectFlip = 0` and `FlipCaps.FlipIndependent = 0` |
| [M65](kmd.md#m65) | kmd | With the UMD name, `DxgkDdiSetStablePowerState`, `SupportDirectFlip`, `FlipCaps.FlipIndependent` and the per-engine TDR set in place (bc250kmd 0.7.4), the lab's dxgkrnl accepts the adapter description and starts building on it: after DRIVERCAPS it takes `GPUMMUCAPS`, four `PAGETABLELEVELDESC` answers, `GetNodeMetadata`, the `QUERYSEGMENT4` table (one CPU-visible local segment over the carve-out, `0x1FD736000` bytes at GPU `0xF4008CA000`), `HISTORYBUFFERPRECISION`, then calls `CreateProcess` (system), `CreateDevice` twice (system) and `CreateContext` (node 0, flags `SystemContext \| VirtualAddressing`), all successful - and then destroys the context, both devices and the process and calls `DxgkDdiStopDevice`, 6 ms after the start; the device shows `CM_PROB_FAILED_POST_START` again. Refused on the way and survived: types 15, 47 and 16 (`DISPLAY_DRIVERCAPS_EXTENSION`). Never entered: `GetRootPageTableSize`, `SetRootPageTable`, `CreateAllocation`, `BuildPagingBuffer`, any VidPN DDI, `ControlInterrupt`. Which check ends the start is NOT known from this run |
| [M66](kmd.md#m66) | kmd | The teardown of M65 is dxgmms2 refusing the paging buffer's segment: `DXGK_QUERYSEGMENTOUT4.PagingBufferSegmentId` must name an aperture segment or be 0 (system memory). Read offline in unit A's `dxgmms2.sys` 10.0.22621.6199: `VidMmInitializePagingProcess` runs `InitDmaPools` right after `VidSchCreateSystemDevices` (the step that made the one `CreateContext` of run 004) and before `InitPagingProcessVaSpace` (the first user of the root page table DDIs); `VIDMM_DMA_POOL::Init` calls `VerifySegmentSet` with required flag bit 0 (`Aperture`) and fails with `STATUS_INVALID_PARAMETER` ("DMA buffer can only be allocated from an aperture segment.") for a named segment without it; a segment set of 0 passes unconditionally. bc250kmd 0.7.4 answered segment 1, flags `0x00080404`, `Aperture` clear. The same reading finds no check that rejects the 0.7.4 `CreateContext` answer |

The display side does not come up: the screen is black, `CommitVidPn` is never reached, no `GetStandardAllocationDriverData`, `CreateAllocation`, `ControlInterrupt`, `SetVidPnSourceAddress` or `Present`; `EnumDisplayDevices` lists no adapter although a monitor devnode is present. The only traffic is `QueryAdapterInfo` type 25 (`ADAPTERPERFDATA`, refused) once a second. Adding `InstalledDisplayDrivers` to the software key changes nothing (run 005c). Why the display side stays down is NOT known from this run; the shared display DDIs did not log |
| [M68](display.md#m68) | display | The dark display of M67 is a refusal, not an omission: with the full table dxgkrnl calls `QueryChildRelations`, `QueryDeviceDescriptor`, `RecommendMonitorModes` and `IsSupportedVidPn` (all answered as the display-only table answers them) and every `EnumVidPnCofuncModality` fails with `0xC01E030A` `STATUS_GRAPHICS_INVALID_FREQUENCY`: modes whose `VSyncFreq`/`HSyncFreq`/`PixelRate` are `D3DKMDT_FREQUENCY_NOTSPECIFIED` are accepted from a display-only driver and not from a full one. With a nominal 60 Hz timing (0.7.7) that refusal is gone |
| [M69](display.md#m69) | display | On a test-signed system the lab's dxgkrnl (10.0.22621.6199) BUGCHECKS on a wrong rotation answer: `VIDEO_DXGKRNL_FATAL_ERROR` 0x113, subtype 0x1C, in `DMMVIDPNPRESENTPATH::SetRotationSupport`, when a driver with path independent rotation (our WDDM 2.0 table) passes `RotationSupport` without `Offset0` for the primary path through `pfnUpdatePathSupportInfo`. bc250kmd 0.7.7 passed `Identity` only and took unit A down twice (the gate was still open at the next boot); the start budget refused the third start as designed. Wrong answers in VidPN callbacks are not always a status: with test signing on, dxgkrnl's driver verification is armed |
| [M70](display.md#m70) | display | With 60 Hz modes and `RotationSupport = Identity \| Offset0` the lab's dxgkrnl accepts the full table's VidPN answers (`EnumVidPnCofuncModality` succeeds) and win32k goes on to create its CDD device and a context with flags `GdiContext \| VirtualAddressing` (0x6). There the machine bugchecks, 0x3B (c0000005) in `dxgmms2!VIDMM_DMA_POOL::AddDmaBufferToPool`: for a GPU-VA context's DMA pool dxgmms2 maps the DMA buffer's VidMm allocation into the context's address space, and with `DXGK_CONTEXTINFO.DmaBufferSegmentSet = 0` (system memory) that allocation pointer is NULL. A GpuMmu driver that answers a non-zero `DmaBufferSize` needs a segment-backed (aperture) DMA buffer; the system paging context (flags 0x5) does not go through this pool and tolerated the same answer |
| [M71](kmd.md#m71) | kmd | With an aperture segment declared (RosKmd's shape: Aperture, CacheCoherent, CpuVisible, 256 MB, nothing behind it) and named in `DmaBufferSegmentSet`, the inert full WDDM 2.0 table (ADR 0008 stage A) runs the desktop's kernel side on unit A end to end: CDD context (flags 0x6), shared primary and shadow surface (`GetStandardAllocationDriverData` types 1 and 2, `CreateAllocation`, `OpenAllocation`), `BuildPagingBuffer` operations 11 (UpdatePageTable), 12 (FlushTlb) and 9 (VirtualFill), `CommitVidPn`, `SetVidPnSourceAddress` (segment 1), `ControlInterrupt` type 3, then `Present` + `SubmitCommandVirtual` pairs whose fences the software completion retires (160 of 160 in 159 s), 0 preemptions, no TDR, no bugcheck, no live kernel report. `MapApertureSegment` is never called. The screen is black, as an inert `Present` and an unprogrammed scanout must leave it. D3D11 hardware device creation fails with 0x887A0004 against the stub UMD, WARP works. Closing the gate brings the display-only table and the picture back in the same boot. Dwminit events pile up while the full table is up (7 to 125 in three minutes), cause not examined |
| [M72](kmd.md#m72) | kmd | What dxgmms2 (10.0.22621) sends in `DXGK_OPERATION_UPDATE_PAGE_TABLE` to a GpuMmu driver that declares `DXGK_PAGETABLEUPDATE_GPU_PHYSICAL`, four levels of 512 entries: (1) `DXGK_PTE.PageAddress` is a page frame number, not a byte address - a level 1 entry with PageAddress 0x1FD732 names the level 0 table VidMm addresses as segment 1 offset 0x1FD732000; for segment 1 it counts from the start of the segment, for host memory it is the physical frame; (2) host memory is `Segment` 0; (3) `PageTableLevel` 0 is the leaf and level 3 is the table later given to `SetRootPageTable`; (4) the page tables of the system paging process are NOT updated in the declared mode: 1028 calls at adapter start arrive with `UpdateMode = DXGK_PAGETABLEUPDATE_CPU_VIRTUAL` and a CPU pointer to the table page (level 0, 512 entries each), before `SetRootPageTable` 1:0x0; process page tables after that arrive as GPU_PHYSICAL; (5) ranges are cleared with `Flags.Repeat` and one invalid entry |
| [M73](kmd.md#m73) | kmd | ADR 0008 stage B's exit criterion is met on unit A. bc250kmd 0.7.12 answers `UpdatePageTable` by translating every `DXGK_PTE` (`driver/shim/bc250_pte.c`: VM leaf flags VALID/READABLE/WRITEABLE/EXECUTABLE with MTYPE NC, SYSTEM for host pages, directory entries VALID only; VRAM addresses as physical, carve-out base + segment offset) and writing it by CPU into the table page - through VidMm's pointer for the paging process (CPU_VIRTUAL), through a mapping of segment offset for everything else. 280643 entries at start, 0 refused. A D3DKMT client (`tools/win/kmtprobe`) asks for GPU VA 0x10000000 and gets it, makes the allocation resident and writes a pattern; the witness, reading VRAM by physical address, finds a four-level chain whose level 1 entry 128 leads to 16 leaves for exactly that range, and the pages they name hold the pattern (first and last page checked). No VMID walks these tables yet: that the GPU agrees with the format is stage C's to show. Also measured: a process without a context never gets `SetRootPageTable`; VidMm allocates page table pages downwards from the top of the segment |
| [M74](kmd.md#m74) | kmd | Installing a driver package over a running full WDDM table does not restart the device: dwm.exe vetoes the removal (Kernel-PnP 225), the device ends in `CM_PROB_FAILED_POST_START` "pending system reboot", and only a restart brings the new package up. With the gate closed (display-only table running) the same install restarts the device in place, as in every run before |
| [M75](kmd.md#m75) | kmd | M6's GPU bring-up is indifferent to which DDI table runs: under the full WDDM 2.0 table (bc250kmd 0.7.12, VidMm owning the segment, page table writes on) `gart enable`, `psp load`, `ih init` and `gfx run 8` return what they returned under the display-only table, gfx fences and a 16-workgroup compute dispatch complete with their interrupts, the undo returns 0, and the full table itself goes on undisturbed (presents, software fences, no TDR). The reserved tail of VRAM and VidMm's segment do not collide |
| [M76](hardware.md#m76) | hardware | The gfx ring executes a `PACKET3_INDIRECT_BUFFER` at VMID 0: the driver's ring test, built as a 3-dword IB in a GTT page and submitted as one IB packet plus one fence (what `amdgpu_ib_schedule()` emits with `job = NULL`; no CONTEXT_CONTROL, no COND_EXEC, no SWITCH_BUFFER), changes the scratch register, and its fence arrives with one end-of-pipe interrupt. The plain fence on the same ring in the same device start is the positive control |
| [M77](kmd.md#m77) | kmd | ADR 0008 stage C runs end to end: a D3DKMT client's command buffer (64 bytes of PM4 NOP at a GPU VA it chose) arrives in `DxgkDdiSubmitCommandVirtual` with `DmaBufferSize` 64; the context had received its own `SetRootPageTable` (M73's missing piece: a context is what triggers it); wddm.c submits it on the gfx ring at VMID 1 after pointing `GCVM_CONTEXT1_PAGE_TABLE_BASE_ADDR` at that root (physical address, M73) and flushing the VMID by MMIO; the fence arrives with exactly one interrupt and one vector, is reported as DMA_COMPLETED, and the client's monitored fence reaches 1. No TDR, no watchdog. Limit of the measurement: NOPs leave no trace, so that the buffer's content was fetched through VidMm's page tables is not shown by run 005; M80 shows it |
| [M78](kmd.md#m78) | kmd | Unit A hung hard (no answer for 19 minutes, no bugcheck event in the next boot, which this session did not start) inside `gfx run 8` when that was the second bring-up of one boot, after two device restarts, with `psp unload` and `gart restore` left out of the first undo. The same driver build and the same steps passed on each of three fresh boots afterwards. The observation is MEASURED (n = 1); the cause is a HYPOTHESIS (compare M55, the second `modprobe amdgpu` of a boot). Until it is measured, a bring-up run starts from a fresh boot and ends with the complete undo |
| [M79](hardware.md#m79) | hardware | The gfx ring's end-of-pipe vector on unit A is client 20 (`SOC15_IH_CLIENTID_GRBM_CP`), source 181, `ring_id` 0, vmid 0, data[0] `0x80000000` - the same for a plain fence, for an IB at VMID 0 and for the WDDM submission whose IB ran at VMID 1 (the fence packet sits in the ring, not in the IB). `ring_id` 0 is what amdgpu decodes as me 0 pipe 0 queue 0. The compute rings' vectors in the same files carry `ring_id` 20 to 23 and data[0] `0x00000001`. The third vector of run 005 is the WDDM submission's, so that interrupt was an end-of-pipe and not a VM fault |
| [M80](kmd.md#m80) | kmd | The CP fetches a process's command buffer through VidMm's page tables at VMID 1 and executes its content: a D3DKMT client's buffer (VidMm allocation, mapped by `MapGpuVirtualAddress` at GPU VA `0x20000000`, page tables written by `vidmm.c` in amdgpu's format, M73) beginning with `PACKET3_SET_UCONFIG_REG` of `mmSCRATCH_REG0` changed that register from `0xDEADBEEF` (read back just before, left there by the driver's own IB ring test in the same device start) to the value in the buffer, `0xBC250B01`, read through bc250kmd's read escape; one end-of-pipe interrupt, fence reported from the hardware path, monitored fence 1, no TDR |
| [M81](kmd.md#m81) | kmd | A Kernel-PnP 411 "had a problem starting" with `Problem Status: 0xC00000E5` (`STATUS_INTERNAL_ERROR`) is written at **every** install of a `bc250kmd` package on unit A, including installs after which the device runs normally, so it is not a signal that a start failed. Six installs, six 411s: driver versions 0.6.1.0, 0.6.2.0, 0.7.0.0, 0.7.1.0, 0.7.2.0 and 0.7.3.0 (`oem13` to `oem18`), each 400 "was configured" record followed within 40 ms by a 411 carrying `Problem: 0x0` - that is, no device problem code. Those are the installs M28, M30, M57, M60 and M63 were measured with. The state file of the last of them records the device `status OK`, `CM_PROB_NONE`, stage 61 forty seconds later. The real refusal of M62/M63 produced **no** 411 at all, only Kernel-PnP 1011 "surprise removed as it was reported to be failing", which carries no status: M63's "Kernel-PnP logs only reported to be failing" stands for the refusal itself |
| [M82](display.md#m82) | display | Under the full table the CDD's presents name their source by GPU virtual address in the allocation list's fixed slots, not through the counters. With `AllocationListSize = DXGK_ALLOCATION_LIST_SIZE_GDICONTEXT` (256) answered to the GDI context (flags `0x6`) every logged `DxgkDdiPresent` (Blt, flags `0x1`, 1 to 5 sub-rectangles, source and destination rectangle both the full 1920 x 1200) still carries `NumSrcAllocations = NumDstAllocations = 0` (run 001 n = 8, run 002 n = 8), while `pAllocationList` is non-NULL and the same pointer in every present. Entry `DXGK_PRESENT_SOURCE_INDEX` reads handle `0`, flags/segment qword `0`, address qword `0x8DC000` in all eight; entry `DXGK_PRESENT_DESTINATION_INDEX` reads all zero; `DmaBufferGpuVirtualAddress` alternates `0x10000` / `0x11000`. The zero handle is this driver's doing: `Bc250WddmOpenAllocation` returns `hDeviceSpecificAllocation = NULL`. INFERENCE, not measured: `0x8DC000 = 0x12000 + 0x8CA000` and `0x8CA000` is one 1920 x 1200 x 4 surface, so the address qword is the source surface's GPU VA in the context's address space, and the zero destination means the primary is implied. Correction from M83: that implied-primary inference is refuted by the measured32-byte pAllocationInfo layout; destination entry2 is the shared-primary allocation. The desktop survives the list: no TDR, no bugcheck, gate closes, picture returns |
| [M83](display.md#m83) | display | The arm of `DXGKARG_PRESENT`'s union that dxgkrnl fills for a GpuMmu driver's Blt present is `pAllocationInfo`: 32-byte `DXGK_PRESENTALLOCATIONINFO` entries, entry 1 the source and entry 2 the destination (entry 0 zero), each with `hDeviceSpecificAllocation` = what `OpenAllocation` returned and `AllocationVirtualAddress` = the surface's GPU VA in the context's address space; `NumSrcAllocations` and `NumDstAllocations` stay 0 throughout. Read as 24-byte `DXGK_ALLOCATIONLIST` entries the same bytes show slot 1 as handle 0 / flags 0 / address 0x8DC000 (M82's reading, now explained). Also: `DxgkCbGetHandleData(DXGK_HANDLE_ALLOCATION)` did not return the object `CreateAllocation` stored for the CDD's handles (0xC00006C0, 0xC0000000: NULL), while `DXGK_OPENALLOCATIONINFO.pPrivateDriverData` carried the driver's own 32-byte blob |
| [M84](display.md#m84) | display | A picture under the full WDDM table: with `EnablePresentBlit` the driver copied each Blt present's sub-rectangles from the source surface (our opened object of M83: 1920 x 1200, `D3DDDIFMT_A8R8G8B8`, pitch 7680, 0x8CA000 bytes, GPU VA 0x8DC000, translated through the GDI context's root page table to one contiguous VRAM range 0x271398000..0x271C61FFF inside segment 1) into the firmware framebuffer by physical address, and the owner at the monitor saw the whole desktop, correct, for the minute the full table ran; no TDR, no bugcheck, gate closed, display-only picture back. The page tables VidMm had the driver write (M73) translate the CDD's surface the way the CPU walk expects. The overlay's GDI screenshot stays the black 3586-byte frame under the full table (it reads the CDD's surfaces, not the scanout) and is not a measure of the picture |
| [M85](display.md#m85) | display | DCN surface address registers hold the SYSTEM PHYSICAL byte address, not the GPU MC address. Firmware (before amdgpu): `HUBPREQ0..3_DCSURF_PRIMARY_SURFACE_ADDRESS` = `0x70000000` with `_HIGH` = `0x2`, i.e. `0x270000000` = the VRAM carve-out base of M31; pitch register `0x77F` (PITCH field = 1919, width-1 in pixels). amdgpu after load: HUBP0 and HUBP3 = `0x46E000000` (fbcon buffer near the top of the carve-out), flips in flip3 alternated `0x46C000000` / `0x46E000000` (amdgpu's `pd_addr`/VRAM values are consistent with VRAM offset 0 = `0x270000000`). Windows conversion for a flip: physical = MC - `0xF400000000` + `0x270000000` |
| [M86](display.md#m86) | display | Firmware lights HUBP0 on OTG0 only: `HUBP0_DCHUBP_CNTL` = `0x000F1002` (NO_OUTSTANDING_REQ, TTU_DISABLE, XRQ_NO_OUTSTANDING_REQ 0xF), HUBP1 = `0x000F101A`, HUBP2 = `0x000F102A`, HUBP3 = `0x000F103A` (IN_BLANK set, VTG_SEL 1/2/3, TTU_DISABLE); `OTG0_OTG_CONTROL` = `0x80011311` (OTG_MASTER_EN, DISABLE_POINT_CNTL 3, START_POINT_CNTL, CURRENT_MASTER_EN_STATE, AVSYNC_VSYNC_N_HSYNC_MODE), `OTG1_OTG_CONTROL` = `0x80000310` (not enabled); `OTG0_OTG_H_TOTAL` = `0x81F` (2080), `OTG0_OTG_V_TOTAL` = `0x4D2` (1235) = 1920x1200 CVT-RB at 59.95 Hz; `HUBP0_DCSURF_SURFACE_CONFIG` = 8 (pixel format 8 = ARGB8888), `DCSURF_ADDR_CONFIG` = 0 (firmware) vs `0x123C` (amdgpu), `DCSURF_TILING_CONFIG` = `0x80` both. amdgpu drives HUBP0 and HUBP3 together on OTG0, 960x1200 each (the DTN log's own ODM 2:1 split, the same layout E13 saw): after load HUBP0 CNTL = `0x000F0102` (VREADY_AT_OR_AFTER_VSYNC instead of TTU_DISABLE), HUBP3 CNTL = `0x000F0102`, HUBP1 = `0x000F101B`. Field names from `dcn_2_0_1_sh_mask.h` |
| [M87](display.md#m87) | display | The page-flip write sequence amdgpu emits on DCN 2.0.1 (from `amdgpu_dm:amdgpu_dc_wreg`, register index x4 = BAR5 byte offset, names via regcalc): `OTG0_OTG_GLOBAL_CONTROL0` = 0; `OTG0_OTG_MASTER_UPDATE_LOCK` = 1; per HUBP (0 and 3): `HUBPREQn_DCSURF_FLIP_CONTROL2` = `0x440` (SURFACE_TRIPLE_BUFFER_ENABLE + PENDING_MIN_TIME 0x40), `DCSURF_FLIP_CONTROL` = `0x200` three times (SURFACE_UPDATE_LOCK 0, SURFACE_FLIP_TYPE 0 = synchronous; `0x200` is the read-only MASTER_UPDATE_LOCK_STATUS echo), `DCSURF_SURFACE_CONTROL` = 0, `DCSURF_PRIMARY_SURFACE_ADDRESS_HIGH`, `DCSURF_PRIMARY_SURFACE_ADDRESS`; then `OTG0_OTG_MASTER_UPDATE_LOCK` = 0, `OTG0_OTG_VUPDATE_KEEPOUT` = 0, `OTG0_OTG_TRIGA_MANUAL_TRIG` = 1. Byte offsets (BAR5): `OTG0_OTG_GLOBAL_CONTROL0` `0x14140`, `OTG0_OTG_MASTER_UPDATE_LOCK` `0x1412C`, `OTG0_OTG_GLOBAL_SYNC_STATUS` `0x14128`, `OTG0_OTG_TRIGA_MANUAL_TRIG` `0x13FE8`, `HUBPREQ0_DCSURF_FLIP_CONTROL` `0x0EB6C`, `_FLIP_CONTROL2` `0x0EB70`, `_SURFACE_CONTROL` `0x0EB68`, `_PRIMARY_SURFACE_ADDRESS` `0x0EB28`, `_HIGH` `0x0EB2C`, `_SURFACE_INUSE` `0x0EB84`, `_SURFACE_FLIP_INTERRUPT` `0x0EB80`, `HUBP0_DCHUBP_CNTL` `0x0EACC`, `HUBPREQ3_DCSURF_PRIMARY_SURFACE_ADDRESS` `0x0F578`. Modeset additionally programmed `OTG0_OTG_VSTARTUP_PARAM` = `0x17`, `OTG_VUPDATE_PARAM` = `0x012F0208`, `OTG_VREADY_PARAM` = `0x18B`, `VTG0_CONTROL` = `0x84D00000`, `HUBP0_DCSURF_ADDR_CONFIG` = `0x123C`, `HUBPRET0_HUBPRET_CONTROL` = `0x00E40000` |
| [M88](display.md#m88) | display | The vblank interrupt this kernel uses on DCN 2.0.1 is `OTG0_IHC_V_UPDATE_NO_LOCK`: IH client_id 4 (DCE), src_id 87 (`0x57`, `DCN_1_0__SRCID__OTG0_IHC_V_UPDATE_NO_LOCK_INTERRUPT`), enabled with bit 12 `VUPDATE_NO_LOCK_INT_EN` and acknowledged with bit 16 `VUPDATE_NO_LOCK_EVENT_CLEAR` of `OTG0_OTG_GLOBAL_SYNC_STATUS` (`0x14128`); 360 such events in the 6 s flip3 trace (one per frame, acks written as `0x0011D104`), 6 in flip2. Neither `DC_D1_OTG_VSTARTUP` (src `0x3C`, `VSTARTUP_INT_EN` bit 0) nor `HUBP0_FLIP_INTERRUPT` (src `0x4F`, `SURFACE_FLIP_INT_MASK` bit 0 of `DCSURF_SURFACE_FLIP_INTERRUPT`, sampled `0x00050000` = FLIP_OCCURRED + FLIP_AWAY_OCCURRED, mask 0) was ever enabled or seen. Reason in source (`ref/linux-src`, not copied into the repo): `amdgpu_dm_crtc.c` `amdgpu_dm_crtc_set_vblank` takes `vupdate_irq` when `amdgpu_ip_version(adev, DCE_HWIP, 0) != 0` (mainline takes `crtc_irq`), and `amdgpu_dm.c` `dm_vupdate_high_irq` completes the page flip when `dc_get_flip_pending_on_otg` says no flip is pending; the pending poll is visible as reads of `HUBPREQ0_DCSURF_FLIP_CONTROL` (2158 reads, value `0x100` = SURFACE_FLIP_PENDING seen 360 times) and `HUBPREQ3_DCSURF_FLIP_CONTROL` (1798 reads, never pending). Other IH traffic during flips: client 8 src 224 and client 9 src 224 (SDMA0/SDMA1 traps) |
| [M89](display.md#m89) | display | `DCSURF_SURFACE_INUSE` read 0 in all 80 samples of flip3 while `PRIMARY_SURFACE_ADDRESS` alternated - it did not follow the address in our BAR5 reads (wishlist L23's control answered negatively; unexplained, possibly the register latches only in a SURFACE_INUSE no-latch read mode) |
| [M90](linux.md#m90) | linux | SMU traffic (wishlist L10, ADR 0010 point 6): a kprobe `p:smumsg smu_cmn_send_smc_msg_with_param msg=%si:u32 param=%dx:u32` saw ONLY `msg=20 param=6` = `SMU_MSG_TransferTableSmu2Dram`, table 6 (the metrics table amdgpu reads for `pp_dpm_sclk`, `gpu_metrics`, hwmon), 61 times in the load run = one per sysfs read of the sampler. Under a 20 s RADV load (vkcube, 11498 CS ioctls, 11498 scheduled jobs on `gfx_0.0.0`, 63 `sdma0` and 26 `sdma1` jobs, 43 VM flushes, +23142 interrupts on irq 54) amdgpu sent no other SMU message: sclk stayed at 1500 MHz (`pp_dpm_sclk` levels 0: 1000, 1: 1500 *, 2: 2000 MHz; mclk/fclk 450 MHz as the sysfs string reads it, which on M789's reading is not the memory data rate, socclk 1254 MHz, `power_dpm_force_performance_level` auto), voltage 906 mV, hwmon `power1_input` 58-62 W, temperature 70-71 C both idle and under load. So on this part the Linux driver does no DPM at all under load; the clock is whatever the SMU boots with (1500 MHz), and the Windows underclock (1000 MHz, 820 mV via `bc250rd`) is a deliberate departure from stock behaviour, not a recovery of it. vkcube needed SIGKILL at the end (futex wait on exit); the GPU stayed healthy |
| [M91](linux.md#m91) | linux | amdgpu's VM flush on the gfx ring for one RADV client: `amdgpu_vm_flush: ring=gfx_0.0.0, id=1, hub=0, pd_addr=046ffe0001` (VMID 1, page directory at system physical `0x46FFE0000` \| 1, VRAM offset `0x1FFFE0000`, corrected 2026-10-06 by M808), SDMA0 flush `id=0 pd_addr=046fe00001` - the same VMID and page-directory address on both rings, consistent with the single process that generated them. Observed only, one client. The two-client half of wishlist L25 is M809 (2026-10-06): one VMID per process, held for the whole run |
| [M92](display.md#m92) | display | Under Windows the DMU registers read through BAR5 at the offsets `gen_regs.py` derives from `dcn_2_0_1_offset.h` (ip DMU, regcalc) give exactly the firmware state E21 measured under Linux before amdgpu (M85, M86): `HUBPREQ0_DCSURF_PRIMARY_SURFACE_ADDRESS` 0x70000000 / `_HIGH` 0x2 (all four HUBPREQs), pitch 0x77F, `HUBP0_DCHUBP_CNTL` 0x000F1002, HUBP1-3 0x000F101A/102A/103A, `OTG0_OTG_CONTROL` 0x80011311, `OTG1_OTG_CONTROL` 0x80000310, `OTG0_OTG_H_TOTAL` 0x81F, `OTG0_OTG_V_TOTAL` 0x4D2, `OTG0_OTG_GLOBAL_SYNC_STATUS` 0x00104104 (no interrupt enabled), `HUBPREQ0_DCSURF_SURFACE_FLIP_INTERRUPT` 0x00050000; `OTG0_OTG_STATUS_FRAME_COUNT` advanced 0x18310 to 0x1834C (60 frames) between two reads about a second apart, so OTG0 scans out at 60 Hz under our display-only driver. The display-only miniport (dxgkrnl's POST path) leaves the firmware's display programming untouched |
| [M93](display.md#m93) | display | The scanned-out picture can be read by the driver independently of GDI: `bc250kmd_cli fbdump` reads HUBP0's surface address from the DCN registers (0x270000000 in display-only mode, M92), maps the surface read-only in 64-row bands and copies 1920 x 1200 x 4 = 9216000 bytes in 396 ms; the frame shows the desktop with the overlay (`mon.py scanout`, half-scale PNG). Needs EnableMmio and EnableVram (the carve-out range); the POST framebuffer address dxgkrnl hands the driver is the BAR alias, not the DCN's system physical address. Side finding: the driver's `UnconfirmedStarts` guard refuses a third device start within ~30 s of a fresh install (the overlay confirms a start only after a healthy desktop), reported as `CM_PROB_FAILED_POST_START` |
| [M94](display.md#m94) | display | The display flip works under Windows with the sequence E21 measured (M87), on HUBP0/OTG0 alone, from the display-only driver: `OTG0_OTG_MASTER_UPDATE_LOCK` 1, `HUBPREQ0_DCSURF_FLIP_CONTROL` 0, `_SURFACE_CONTROL` 0, `_PRIMARY_SURFACE_ADDRESS_HIGH`/`_ADDRESS`, lock 0, `OTG0_OTG_TRIGA_MANUAL_TRIG` 1. The address register moved 0x270000000 -> 0x271000000 -> 0x270000000, `SURFACE_FLIP_PENDING` (0x100) cleared after 16.1 / 12.1 / 13.5 ms (one frame at 59.95 Hz), `OTG0_OTG_STATUS_FRAME_COUNT` kept advancing, `HUBP_UNDERFLOW_STATUS` stayed 0, and the scanout read back through M93's instrument while the address was 0x271000000 was the CPU-filled surface (blue field, white border, diagonal). `DCSURF_SURFACE_INUSE` stayed 0 throughout (as on Linux, M89). `GLOBAL_CONTROL0`, `VUPDATE_KEEPOUT` and `FLIP_CONTROL2` were left at the firmware's values. What the monitor showed during the 8 s is not recorded (owner absent) |
| [M95](hardware.md#m95) | hardware | SDMA0 executes the `SDMA_OP_CONST_FILL` and `SDMA_OP_COPY`/`COPY_LINEAR` packets transcribed from amdgpu's sdma_v5_0 (`driver/shim/bc250_sdma_copy.c`) under Windows, from the bring-up rings of M53-M60: fill(source, 0xA5) -> copy(source -> destination) -> fence on 4096 and on 65536 bytes between two VRAM regions (MC 0xF5FE00B000 and 0xF5FE01B000) gave a destination of 0xA5 in every byte (the source had been seeded with counting bytes and the destination with 0xEE by CPU, so a skipped fill or a skipped copy would have shown), fences 1 and 2 read back, 11-12 us from the CPU seed to the read-back, after the SDMA0 ring-test fence (4 us) as the control; gfx fini, psp unload and gart restore afterwards left the display-only driver presenting |
| [M96](kmd.md#m96) | kmd | bc250kmd 0.7.24 with the paging node gate open (`EnablePagingNode` 1) and the full table live, on the bring-up path that E19 run 003 had driven to the end on 0.7.13 (gart enable, psp load, ih init, then `gfx run 8`), hung unit A completely inside the `gfx run 8` escape: no output line of that phase was written, the ssh session died inside the call, both network addresses stopped answering and the monitor went black, with no bugcheck, no TDR event and no live kernel report - a hard hang needing the power button. What is new since the run that worked is 0.7.23's SDMA changes (`Sdma0RingLock`, the interlocked fence sequence, the split ring test) and that VidMm's paging operations could reach node 1's hardware path during the bring-up; which of the two did it is not yet established |
| [M97](display.md#m97) | display | `DxgkDdiSetVidPnSourceAddress` performs the display flip under Windows from the full WDDM table (bc250kmd 0.7.24, `EnableVidPnFlip`): dxgkrnl passed the CDD's primary at card address 0xF400ACE000, the driver converted it to system physical 0x270ACE000 (M85's arithmetic) and ran M87's write sequence; HUBP0's address register read the converted value afterwards and the driver's own scanout read-back showed that surface, not the POST framebuffer. One flip served 60 presents: dxgkrnl called the address DDI once (at the source becoming visible) and sent every later frame through `Present`, so the CPU blit of E20 kept painting the POST framebuffer that was no longer being scanned out, and the visible picture was the CDD's own partially painted surface. 0 flips refused, none arrived above DISPATCH_LEVEL, no TDR, no bugcheck |
| [M98](display.md#m98) | display | The OTG0 VUPDATE_NO_LOCK interrupt does not reach the driver while the IH ring is not running: in the same run the driver armed it (`OTG0_OTG_GLOBAL_SYNC_STATUS` written 0x00115104, the enable bit of M88) and over about 40 s of visible desktop at 59.95 Hz the ISR saw it 0 times (0 acked, 0 deferred, 0 reported to dxgkrnl), with `EnableIh` closed and the IH ring never initialised for that device start. The arming itself and the rest of the run were clean. The inference that the IH ring is the missing link - amdgpu delivers DMU interrupts through IH (client 4 source 0x57, M88) - is not yet measured; the experiment that settles it is the same run with the engines gate open and `ih init` done first |
| [M99](display.md#m99) | display | The OTG0 VUPDATE_NO_LOCK interrupt does not arrive even with the IH ring running, so M98's inference was wrong: in E22 run 004 the same arming write (`OTG0_OTG_GLOBAL_SYNC_STATUS` = 0x00115104, bit 12) was made with `EnableIh` open and the bring-up's `gart enable`, `psp load` and `ih init` all successful (ring ENABLED, MSI vector 0x70), and after 40 s of visible desktop at 59.95 Hz the escape read: interrupt routine called 0 times since the device started, 0 interrupts taken as ours, 0 vectors consumed, 0 overflows, rptr and wptr both 0. The same MSI path does deliver the CP's interrupts (M77), so what is missing is the route from DMU to the IH ring, not the ring and not the MSI |
| [M100](display.md#m100) | display | The surface `SetVidPnSourceAddress` flips to is not the one anything draws into: in E22 runs 003 and 004 the scanned-out primary (card 0xF400ACE000, physical 0x270ACE000) read back as uninitialised VRAM with the remains of E22 run 002's own test fill inside it (that fill lies at 0x271000000, which falls within the 9.2 MB primary), while the `Present` calls of the same runs named a different allocation as their source (physical 0x271398000, the CDD's rendering target) and the CPU blit of E20 copied it into the POST framebuffer at the firmware's address, which nothing scans out any more once the flip has run. So a present under the full table has to put the source where the scanout is, and the scanout has to be the allocation dxgkrnl names as the primary |
| [M101](kmd.md#m101) | kmd | The full WDDM table with the paging node open runs the whole bring-up on this part, when the run is the first of its boot: E24 run 003 (bc250kmd 0.7.25, `EnableFullWddm`, `EnableGpuVa`, `EnableGpuSubmit`, `EnablePresentBlit`, `EnablePagingNode`, `EnableVidPnFlip`, the engine gates) took `gart enable`, `psp load`, `ih init` and then `gfx run 1` to `gfx run 8` as eight separate escape calls, all eight returning STATUS_SUCCESS (CP 346 us, SDMA 109 us, interrupt sources 23 us), with dxgkrnl asking for node 1's metadata and creating a context on it, 170 presents and 169 CPU blits served meanwhile, no TDR, no bugcheck and a clean undo. The two hangs of the same day (run 001 inside `gfx run 8`, run 002 inside stage 6, the CP) were both the second bring-up of their boot, which is the shape fact M78 named after E19 run 002 - so M78 now stands at n = 3 and its rule holds: one bring-up per boot |
| [M102](kmd.md#m102) | kmd | An idle desktop gives the paging node nothing to do: in the same run VidMm called `BuildPagingBuffer` 1079 times for operation 11, 8 times for 12 and twice for 9, and not once for `VIRTUAL_TRANSFER` or `VIRTUAL_FILL`, so node 1 reported 0 hardware submissions, 0 completions, 0 timeouts and 0 refusals while being advertised and holding a context of its own. The node's hardware path is therefore still unmeasured; what is measured is that advertising it and letting dxgkrnl build page-table operations on it costs nothing |
| [M104](kmd.md#m104) | kmd | A `struct amdgpu_device` is 0x5B00 bytes (23,296) and must never be a local in a driver: `bc250_sdma_paging_copy()`/`fill()` each declared one, and the first call VidMm ever made into that path bugchecked unit A with 0x50 PAGE_FAULT_IN_NONPAGED_AREA at `nt!_chkstk+0x36` (`sub rsp,rax`, rax 0x5C00). The thread was `dxgmms2!VidMmWorkerThreadProc`, stack base `...362a000` limit `...3624000` (24,576 bytes, the x64 kernel default) with 0x12D0 already spent by eight dxgmms2 frames, so the probe reached 0x1000 below the limit. The fix passes the live `adev` in (0.7.26); `tools/win/stackbudget.py` reads every function's fixed frame out of the image's own unwind data and fails the build at 4096 bytes - it reports those two functions at 23,576 bytes in the 0.7.25 binary and 872 as the whole driver's largest in 0.7.26 |
| [M105](kmd.md#m105) | kmd | VidMm does drive the paging node once memory is tight, and it arrives as a FILL, not a transfer: 512 MB held by `kmtprobe` made dxgmms2 call `VIDMM_GLOBAL::FillAllocationUsingGpuVa` -> `dxgkrnl!ADAPTER_RENDER::DdiBuildPagingBuffer` -> our `Bc250WddmBuildPagingBuffer` -> `GfxPagingBuild`, on the VidMm worker thread, under `PageInOneAllocation` -> `FindResourcesForOneAllocation` -> `CommitResource` -> `TransferToSegment`. This is the traffic M102 said an idle desktop never produces. What the packets then did on SDMA0 is still unmeasured: the same call bugchecked on M104 before a packet was built |
| [M106](display.md#m106) | display | The IH ring does deliver DCE vectors on this part: in E24 run 004, with the ring up and the eight stages done, `ih state` went from "called 0 times, 0 vectors, rptr 0x0 wptr 0x0" before a CP fence to "called 202 times, 202 taken as ours, 255 DPCs, 203 vectors consumed, 0 overflows, rptr 0x1960 wptr 0x1960" about 6 s later, and both vectors the tool printed are `client 4 source 87 ring 0 vmid 0 pasid 0` - the VUPDATE_NO_LOCK identity of M88, not the CP fence's client 20 source 181 (M79). So the vupdate interrupt reaches the ISR when the ring is running; M98's and M103's zero counts were device starts with nothing to deliver through. Only the last two of the 203 vectors were printed, so what the other 201 were is not measured. Run 005 repeated it at scale in its own device start: 3122 interrupts, 3122 taken as ours, 3550 DPCs, 3123 vectors, 0 overflows over about 120 s of a live flip, and the two vectors printed there are the same `client 4 source 87` |
| [M107](kmd.md#m107) | kmd | The paging node builds real packets for real VidMm traffic, and the M104 fix holds: in E24 run 005 (bc250kmd 0.7.26, one bring-up of a fresh boot, `kmtprobe` holding 64 then 256 then 512 MB) VidMm sent four `DXGK_OPERATION_VIRTUAL_FILL`s and the driver answered all four - 64 MB at physical 0x271C62000 in 80 dwords, then 256 MB there in 320 dwords twice, then 256 MB at 0x281C62000 in 320 dwords - 872,415,232 bytes in all, 0 transfers, 0 insufficient-buffer, 0 refusals for any of the five unsupported reasons. So `VidMmTranslate` resolves a live VidMm GPU VA to a physical address (the 64 MB one is the very address the run 004 dump carried in r8), `PagingRangeContiguous` walks 65,536 pages of a 256 MB operation and finds them contiguous, and the emitters produce one CONST_FILL per 4 MB. 512 MB arrives as two 256 MB fills at addresses 256 MB apart. No TDR, no bugcheck, clean undo, 67-72 C |
| [M108](kmd.md#m108) | kmd | dxgkrnl builds those paging buffers and never submits them: across the whole of E24 run 005 `DxgkDdiSubmitCommand` - the physical submit DDI, the one a paging buffer takes (`wddm.c` `Bc250WddmSubmitCommand`) - was not called once, it does not appear in the DDI tally at all, and node 1's own counters end at `0 hardware submitted, 0 completed, 0 timeouts, 0 refused`. The only submissions in the run are 134 `DxgkDdiSubmitCommandVirtual` calls, all node 0, all `size 0`. So nothing of what the node built ever reached SDMA0, and H2's hardware half and H4 stay untested for a reason that is now in dxgkrnl's court rather than ours: either a cap we report wrong, or VidMm discarding an operation whose allocation was never waited on |
| [M109](display.md#m109) | display | The hardware vsync route is complete on this part, end to end, and it has been running unnoticed since 0.7.24: in E24 run 005's own summary the flip path reports `1 hardware flips, 0 refused, 1 hardware vsyncs armed 2784 acked 0 refused 0 deferred` over 120 s with one live flip, and `0 ticks, 286 reported to dxgkrnl`. So OTG0's VUPDATE_NO_LOCK reaches the IH ring (M106), the ISR acknowledges it at DIRQL 2784 times, the DPC finds `SURFACE_FLIP_PENDING` clear every single time (0 deferred) and calls `DxgkCbNotifyInterrupt(DXGK_INTERRUPT_CRTC_VSYNC)` 286 times - every tick for which dxgkrnl had the interrupt enabled through `ControlInterrupt`, which it toggles (1060 calls in the run). Not one of the 286 came from the software timer: `VSyncTicks` is 0, and that counter is the only thing the timer increments |
| [M110](kmd.md#m110) | kmd | A paging buffer on this machine is submitted through `DxgkDdiSubmitCommandVirtual`, not `DxgkDdiSubmitCommand`, because VidMm creates node 1's paging system context with virtual addressing: E24 run 006 logs `wddm: CreateContext node 1 engine 0x1 flags 0x00000005`, and in `DXGK_CREATECONTEXTFLAGS` (`d3dkmddi.h` line 1512) bit 0 is `SystemContext` and bit 2 is `VirtualAddressing` (line 1521). Three measurements in the same run agree: `SubmitCommand` is absent from the DDI tally across 110 s and 1581 `BuildPagingBuffer` calls; `D3DKMTMakeResident` returned `STATUS_PENDING` with a paging fence for each of the three probes and every probe then completed, so the fence was retired by something, and with no hardware submission and no `SubmitCommand` the only remaining path is the software completion at the end of `Bc250WddmSubmitCommandVirtual`; and `SubmitCommandVirtual` runs ahead of the `Present` count by exactly one per probe (70/70, 83/82, 104/102, 122/119 - four fills, three submissions, because two of the fills were packed into one buffer). `DXGKARG_SUBMITCOMMANDVIRTUAL` (line 5218) carries no submission start/end offsets, only `DmaBufferVirtualAddress`, so the offset into the driver's shadow copy can only be recovered against `DXGKARG_BUILDPAGINGBUFFER::DmaBufferGpuVirtualAddress` (line 5071), "GPU virtual address of the start of the DMA buffer". Corrected by M112: on this build that field is 0 for the system paging buffer, and `DmaBufferVirtualAddress` on the submission is already the byte offset |
| [M111](kmd.md#m111) | kmd | The `pDmaBuffer` advance of M108 works, and VidMm packs several paging operations into one buffer: in E24 run 006 the four fills are built at shadow offsets 0x0, 0x140, 0x640 and 0xB40, each starting exactly where the previous ended (80 dwords, then 320 dwords twice), where every fill of run 005 was built at 0x0. dxgkrnl counts the same bytes from its own side - the call at 83.898 s reports `65216 bytes free` of a 65536-byte paging buffer, 65536 minus the 320 bytes of the fill before it |

Node 1 ended at 0 hardware submitted, 0 switches, shadow "0x0 to 0x0". The IH control before the pressure did move (a CP fence, 0 vectors to 2). No TDR, no bugcheck, clean undo, 67-73 C |

The fence slot still read 0 at 500 ms, the path closed (`seq 3 in flight, slot 0x0`), and the later two submissions never ran: 1 hardware submitted, 0 completed, 1 timeout. Leaf low 12 bits `0x071` are VALID, EXECUTABLE, READABLE and WRITEABLE, and not SYSTEM (`AMDGPU_PTE_*`). The IB page on the same submit is leaf `0x14B120073`, which is those flags plus SYSTEM. The four zero dwords are not a VRAM read: `VidMmProbeIb` copies bytes only for a system page. `ih state` before teardown consumed 7 vectors. VMID 1 has client 0x1b (`SOC15_IH_CLIENTID_UTCL2`) source 0 (`UTCL2_1_0__SRCID__FAULT`) and client 0x0a (`SOC15_IH_CLIENTID_SE0SH`) source 239 (`GFX_10_1__SRCID__SQ_INTERRUPT_ID`), 44 ns apart. The SQ payload `00100000 00000080` has encoding bits 38:39 equal to 2 (`SQ_INTERRUPT_WORD_ENCODING_ERROR`) and `ERR_TYPE` bits 19:22 equal to 2. No header consulted here names error type 2. No VMID 1 CP end-of-pipe (source 181). The fault payload was not decoded as an address. No TDR (Display 4101 = 0), no bugcheck, undo back to stage 61 display-only, UnconfirmedStarts confirmed 0. The shader fetch page is mapped. A wave ran, and VMID 1 faulted. M8 is not reached |

Changing address32_hi to the actual heap's high word (1), retaining KMD 0.7.48.1 and the job frame, lets full fill_g1 finish in 1140.3 us with CPU hash 0x7018cdd513a22325. The subsequent full suite has no UTCL2/SQ fault. |

| [M583](icd.md#m583) | icd | The 44 CTS cases dEQP-VK.api.external.semaphore.opaque_win32.* and .opaque_win32_kmt.* (cts-release-tools deqp-vk AE7BEFDD, one process per case, loader debug log kept) give the same result on the registered ICD 93B1D1FD and on the fence-share candidate 6661C2D2: 4 Pass (the info_binary and info_timeline queries), 40 NotSupported ("Semaphore doesn't support exporting in external type"), 0 Fail, 0 Crash, 0 timeout; every process loaded wsi-final/vulkan_radeon.dll. Cause of the NotSupported, read from the info logs and the runtime source: the exportable and importable bits are advertised only for timeline semaphores with OPAQUE_WIN32 or D3D12_FENCE (vk_wddm2_monitored_fence_type exports NT handles), binary semaphores use the vk_sync_binary wrapper, which has no import or export, and OPAQUE_WIN32_KMT is never advertised by vk_semaphore.c. The 40 functional cases are binary-semaphore tests, so the CTS exercises the fence-share change only through the info query; its runtime witness stays M581 (vkd3d-proton D3D12_FENCE export). Run 001 of the same list was void: this deqp-vk build rejects --deqp-watchdog-total-time-limit and --deqp-watchdog-interval-time-limit (exit -1 on every case). |

| [M610](games.md#m610) | games | The 6x slower CPU present copy of E47 (M607) is process-specific and is not explained by the memory type the ICD requests and receives, by memory pressure, by page faults or by scheduling; the effective CPU mapping attribute and the physical placement of the game's buffer remain open. Same boot, same KMD 153, CPU DWM 4596, within one hour: vkcube on the CPU present path copies a 1920x1181 image in 1.07 ms (8.5 GB/s) on the host-cached candidate 50E99A84, on E43's 6661C2D2 and on the witness 63AF86CB, and a 640x480 image in 0.14 ms (E46 level); the steered Witcher 3 DX12 scene copies 1920x1200 in 18.3 ms median (run 009, 3201 presents) and 17.2 ms median (run 010, 2688 presents) with a floor of 16.9-17.9 ms and no value near vkcube's. The witness ICD shows the blit buffer in RADV memory type 5, properties 0xe (HOST_VISIBLE, COHERENT, CACHED) in both processes, and QueryThreadCycleTime around the copy gives 54.3 million cycles per game frame against 3.45 million in vkcube for the same byte count, CPU share 1.00 in both (0.17 versus 2.6 bytes per cycle). The game's page faults are 0-22/s in the scene (side sampler). 50E99A84's HOST_CACHED request was a no-op because the port sets wsi->sw and upstream already selects a cached type; with sw the WSI waits for the image fence before the present, so copy time is memcpy time. Open: the physical placement (segment) and Lock2 cache attribute of the game's 9.2 MB blit buffer, not visible from the ICD (HYPOTHESIS: uncached BAR or non-aperture placement in the game process; 0.5 GB/s is the shape of an uncached read stream). Registered ICD 93B1D1FD restored after every stage, smoke PASS, no KMD change. |

Kernel driver 0.7.196.1 (`28b89a4c`, SYS `22FDE5B8`, registered 2026-10-02T20:05Z) replaces the sleep with a 500 us spin in 20 us steps and then a wait on the graphics retirement event with a 1 ms fallback. In the one pair that changes nothing but the kernel driver (314 against 318: shell `A27C9617`, engine `D4057452`, ICD `102D77EC`, high preset) 31.3 becomes 36.8 frames/s, +17.6 %, with idle 5.83 -> 1.18 ms per frame against a frame time 31.95 -> 27.17 ms, graphics idle 18.3 % -> 4.3 % of the window, and the pipeline time per frame unchanged (25.54 -> 25.46 ms). At low the rate rises about 9 % (54.3-54.8 to 59.6), but that pair does not hold the shell, engine and ICD identical and its rates carry the cost of the ETW kernel session itself |
