| [M785](hardware.md#m785) | hardware | The firmware accepts a GFX clock below the 1000 MHz level that its power tables publish. In session 402 the thermal cap put the part at 800 MHz / VID 116 (820 mV) and at 900 MHz / VID 116. That session ran the Rise of the Tomb Raider DirectX 12 benchmark on kernel driver 0.7.205.1, `DpmMaxMHz` 1500. The SMU refused neither point. The clock readback stream holds 49 samples at 800 MHz and 4 at 900 MHz. The 800 MHz samples run from 81.875 to 87.750 C, mean 84.55 C. The 900 MHz samples read 81.500 to 81.750 C. The governor's own telemetry and summary lines report the same two points 22 and 2 times. Of those 24 lines, 22 read `want 1000 cap 800` and one `want 1200 cap 900`, all 23 with `throttle thermal-soft`. The last line reads `want 1100 cap 1100 throttle thermal-ramp`. The SMU readback was still 900 MHz there while the cap had already risen. GPU busy on the 24 lines runs from 90.3 to 100.0 %. The log holds no `sub-floor refused` line. So 1000 MHz is the lowest level the tables offer (M47) and amdgpu's own clamp (`CYAN_SKILLFISH_SCLK_MIN`). It is not a measured hardware floor |
| [M786](hardware.md#m786) | hardware | CPU power management on unit A under Windows 11 build 22631, idle desktop, Balanced scheme `381b4222-f694-41f0-9685-ff5bb260df2e`. The processor runs its own P-states (`PERFAUTONOMOUS` 1 on AC) inside the window `PROCTHROTTLEMIN` 5 % to `PROCTHROTTLEMAX` 100 %. The other scheme values are `PERFEPP` 33 %, `PERFBOOSTMODE` 2 (aggressive), `PERFBOOSTPOL` 60 % and no frequency ceiling (`PROCFREQMAX` 0). Idle states are allowed (`IDLEDISABLE` 0, `IDLESTATEMAX` 0). No core is parked on AC (`CPMINCORES` and `CPMAXCORES` 100 %, against 10 % and 100 % on DC). Over 20 idle samples at 1 s: `% of Maximum Frequency` mean 46.00 (42.00 to 100.00), `% Processor Performance` mean 62.66 (42.98 to 106.15), `% Processor Time` mean 3.33 %. Windows reports the part as `AMD BC-250`, 6 cores, 12 logical processors, nominal 3194 MHz. `Processor Frequency` read 3194 in all 20 samples, so that counter gives the nominal frequency and not the live one |
| [M787](hardware.md#m787) | hardware | Reported by the community, not measured here. Unit A's video block (VCN 2.0.3, IP discovery hardware id 12, instance 0) is present and not harvested (`harvest = 0`). The firmware keeps its island power- and clock-gated. A kernel built to attach to it wedges the machine at the first VCN MMIO access in `vcn_v2_0_hw_init`. The report shows 10 IP blocks up, SMU 88.6.0 answering, then silence. The PSP refuses the VCN firmware load (`fw_type` 13 = VCN0 in the PSP `t28` blob) with `ITEM_NOT_FOUND`. The cold-reset write that a working part gets from the PSP therefore never happens |
| [M788](display.md#m788) | display | The DisplayPort reference clock on unit A is 600.000 MHz, read from the hardware. `CLK4_0_CLK4_CLK2_CURRENT_CNT` (byte offset `0x6C9FC` by `regcalc` over `clk_11_0_1_offset.h`, 100 kHz units) reads `0x1770` = 6000 in three reads on an idle desktop under Windows. The `dcn` dump holds OTG0 and OTG1. OTG0 reads `OTG_CONTROL` `0x80011311`, master enable set. Its `OTG_H_TOTAL` field is 2079 (h_total 2080) and its `OTG_V_TOTAL` field 1234 (v_total 1235), the same values M86 read under Linux. OTG1 reads `OTG_CONTROL` `0x80000310`, master enable clear, with h_total, v_total and frame count 0, so the second timing generator is present and dark. A community board read the same counter at the same offset (dword index `0x1B27F`) and got the same 6000. That board was tracking a kernel audio defect with two causes. One was a nominal 730 MHz reference in the wrong clock manager. The other was a VBIOS spread-spectrum flag that this board's clock does not honour |
| [M789](hardware.md#m789) | hardware | The `mclk and fclk 450 MHz` that amdgpu reports on unit A (M47, M90) is not the memory data rate. The GDDR6 on this board runs at 1750 MHz with `tCL` 24. A 256-bit bus at 14 Gbps gives about 448 GB/s of peak bandwidth. That puts our measured 386-389 GB/s copy (M776) at about 86 % of peak. The 450 MHz figure is a misreport of the same state, and not a low memory state |
| [M790](hardware.md#m790) | hardware | Reported by the community, not measured here. A board-level over-current protection hard-locks the whole BC-250 in a clock band. On 40-CU boards that band starts between about 1850 and 2200 MHz, and it catches every tested board at 2400 MHz. In that state the monitor goes to standby and the reset and power buttons do nothing. Only cutting AC recovers the board. The reporters call it a system-wide power limit and not GPU-specific. A higher CPU overclock lowers the GPU voltage threshold at which the board hard-locks. Defective CUs clock to 2600 MHz on their own. So the limit they report is global power or VRM, not clock capability |
| [M791](hardware.md#m791) | hardware | Our SMU mailbox is the firmware's own queue 0, from a third-party static read of the firmware. Its descriptor table gives queue 0 the command, argument and response mailboxes at SMN `0x03B10A08`, `0x03B10A48` and `0x03B10A68`. The low 12 bits of those three addresses are `0xA08`, `0xA48` and `0xA68`. `regcalc` computes the byte offsets `0x58A08` for `mmMP1_SMN_C2PMSG_66`, `0x58A48` for `mmMP1_SMN_C2PMSG_82` and `0x58A68` for `mmMP1_SMN_C2PMSG_90`, whose low 12 bits are those same three values. Those are the three registers our transport uses, in the same three roles. All five messages we send have a non-zero handler entry in queue 0's table: `GetSmuVersion` `0x02`, `RequestGfxclk` `0x0E`, `GetGfxFrequency` `0x37`, `GetGfxVid` `0x38` and `ForceGfxVid` `0x3B` |
| [M792](d3d.md#m792) | d3d | An app-local DXVK `dxgi.dll` shadows the System32 one for the System32 modules a Vulkan WSI loads, and the shadow breaks exactly one call (E56, six harness modes, development PC, Windows 10.0.26200, NVIDIA user-mode driver). In a process that first loaded DXVK 3.1.1 `dxgi.dll` (`2E674A56`) by name, System32 `d3d11.dll` loaded afterwards by full path binds its only static `dxgi` import, `CreateDXGIFactory2`, to the app-local DLL, and `GetModuleHandleW` for the name `dxgi.dll` answers with the app-local DLL as well. A D3D11 device on an explicit adapter from the System32 factory still works, and its factory, adapter, device, `IDXGIDevice`, NT and KMT shared textures and shared `ID3D11Fence` are all implemented in System32. Only `D3D11CreateDevice` with a NULL adapter fails. It enters the DXVK factory, DXVK builds a Vulkan instance and reports the GPU as 1002:73df, DXVK refuses the private interface query of `d3d11`, and the call returns 0x8000FFFF with exception 0xC0000005. `LOAD_LIBRARY_SEARCH_SYSTEM32` on the load of `d3d11` does not change that result |
| [M793](d3d.md#m793) | d3d | The DXGI composition present route that a Vulkan WSI needs passes beside app-local DXVK and vkd3d-proton DLLs, so the plan needs no copy of any System32 DLL (E56, six modes, development PC, 2026-09-28). The route is a System32 `CreateDXGIFactory2`, `EnumAdapterByLuid` on the wanted LUID, a presenter and a producer D3D11 device on that adapter, an NT shared BGRA8 texture, a cross-device shared fence, `DCompositionCreateDevice` with a visual, `CreateSwapChainForComposition` (FLIP_SEQUENTIAL, two buffers), four frames of `CopyResource` and `Present1(1,0)` against the fence, `ResizeBuffers` to 320x200, and one `ALLOW_TEARING` swap chain with `Present1(0, ALLOW_TEARING)`. It passes in a clean directory, beside DXVK `d3d11` (`41CE2364`) and `dxgi` (`F593D6C2`), and beside DXVK `dxgi` (`2E674A56`) with vkd3d-proton `d3d12` (`7B77ED5C`) and `d3d12core` (`90B1DAD6`), each of the three without and with a seal policy. Every mode exits 0 in 0.28 s to 0.33 s, no mode produced a DXVK log, frame 0 reads back BGRA 191,127,64,255 against the expected 191,128,64,255, and the view the game has of its own modules does not change. `CreateSwapChainForComposition` and `Present1` are implemented by System32 `dxgi` in every shadowed mode |
| [M794](d3d.md#m794) | d3d | What the shadow of M792 reaches in the System32 binaries of this Windows build (`dxgi` 10.0.26100.9444, `d3d11`, `dcomp` and `d3d12` 10.0.26100.9278), read from the binaries and confirmed by the runtime import reports of E56. System32 `d3d11.dll` has exactly one static `dxgi` import, `CreateDXGIFactory2`, with its import address table at 0x1801EBD30. At device creation `d3d11` calls `GetModuleHandleA` for the name DXGI and then `GetProcAddress` for `CompatValue`, at 0x180008C21 to 0x180008C3E through the import slots 0x1801EB8A8 and 0x1801EB8C0. The app-local DXVK `dxgi.dll` exports five names only, so `CompatValue` resolves to nothing under the shadow and `d3d11` keeps its own defaults. `d3d11` delay-imports `d3d11on12.dll!OpenAdapter_D3D11On12`, and System32 `dcomp` delay-imports `CreateDXGIFactory1`, `CreateDXGIFactory2` and `D3D11CreateDevice`. All of those stay unresolved through the whole route. System32 `dxgi` holds bare-name `LoadLibraryW` call sites for `d3d11.dll` at 0x18002876C and 0x1800A14C9 and for `d3d12.dll` at 0x18009482D, and the 17 distinct loader calls the route makes from System32 graphics modules reach none of them |

| [M796](hardware.md#m796) | hardware | Unit A carries a Nuvoton NCT6686D hardware monitor, and Linux reads the case fan through it. Its EC window is the I/O range `0x0A20-0x0A2F`, which the firmware gives to a PnP motherboard-resources node that drives nothing. The chip publishes five tachometer channels and five duty outputs. One fan turns: `fan2` at 1589 RPM, with `fan1` and `fan3` to `fan5` at 0. Every duty read-back is 245 of 255 (96 %). At the same moment `k10temp` read Tctl 83.5 C and amdgpu read edge 82.0 C, so the chip's own TSI channel is a second, independent reading of the APU die |
| [M797](d3d.md#m797) | d3d | The long idle gaps of the 3D ring (node 0) in game sessions 418, 419 and 420 are a refusal in dxgkrnl's software ready set, not a missing wake-up and not a gap in our completion path. Conjecture C48 (the next packet waits for the VSync interrupt's DPC) dies on clause 1 of its own pre-registered kill rule in 418 of 418 stalls of session 418: dxgkrnl's scheduler worker thread dispatches the packet that ends the stall, a median 121 us after the VSync interrupt and 108 us after the VSync DPC, so no DPC dispatched it. Our kernel driver reports `DXGK_INTERRUPT_DMA_COMPLETED` for 100 % of the 19694 gap boundaries and retires exactly one packet per report. What follows is dxgkrnl: the completion sets the stalling context to status `0x0000000a` (never at a fast boundary, 0 of 16593), the scheduler worker wakes a median 19 us later and its node-0 `SelectContext2` reports `ReadyNodeSwMapBits` bit 0 CLEAR while `ReadyNodeHwMapBits` bit 0 is SET, it selects nothing and parks on the VSync event, and an adapter-wide context-status sweep after the VSync sets the context to `0x00000005` a median 23 us before the submit (404 of 418). The stall holds a dispatchable packet for 95.4 % to 96.9 % of its length, a median 8 ms, and the class costs 0.45 ms to 0.67 ms of each frame |
| [M798](d3d.md#m798) | d3d | The refusal of M797 looks like the effect of a `D3DKMTEscape` with `Flags.HardwareAccess = 1`, which makes dxgkrnl suspend the whole GPU scheduler. While the suspend is in force, a DMA completion parks the game's context at status `0x0a` instead of making it ready, and only the escape's resume lifts it. One such escape can cost up to one VSync period, because dxgmms2's suspend path submits two global commands that only its VSync-paced worker retires. Our own escape handler is not the cost: over the long holds it runs for less than 1 % of the hold and waits for dxgmms2's worker in 167 of 168 cases. In session 430 the suspending client was the lab's own overlay poll, six `bc250kmd_cli.exe` children of `bc250mon.exe`, one every 5.8 s to 6.4 s, each issuing 131 to 191 Level Two escapes, and 200 of 239 long stalls plus 112 of 113 VSync-ended ones fell inside those six spans (14.2 % of the window) |
| [M799](d3d.md#m799) | d3d | Stopping the lab overlay's summary poll removes the VSync-ended long-stall class of M797 on unit A. | [M808](linux.md#m808) | linux | Page table entries that amdgpu writes on unit A, read back out of VRAM and checked against a positive control and against the tracepoint (E17, 2026-10-06, the first run of the kit). A sparse (PRT) 4 KB mapping is the leaf `0x0088000000000006` (SYSTEM, SNOOPED, PRT bit 51, LOG bit 55, not VALID, address 0). A 2 MB PRT mapping is one PDE_PTE entry at level 2, `0x00c8000000000486`, FRAG 9. `AMDGPU_VM_PAGE_NOALLOC` reaches a GTT leaf as bit 58 (`0x0400000107c96067`). 64 KB is FRAG 4, and 2 MB GTT and VRAM mappings are PDE_PTE entries at level 2 with FRAG 9. Directory entries carry VALID alone, and VM leaves default to MTYPE NC. Hypotheses H1 to H7 all hold: 10 of 10 witness words read back at the address that their own entry names, 45 of 45 walked entries equal the traced `amdgpu_vm_set_ptes` values, a VA at 512 GB goes through root entry 1, and `GCVM_CONTEXT1..15_PAGE_TABLE_BASE_ADDR` stay 0 while nothing is submitted. The page table addresses inside entries are system physical (VRAM offset plus `vram_base_offset` `0x270000000`), while the tracepoint's `pe` is the MC address in the `0xF400000000` window |

Initialization then takes 4.4 to 19.0 ms, events come 10.13 to 10.64 ms apart and no wait times out |
| [M831](kmd.md#m831) | kmd | KMD 0.7.216.3 (branch `kmd/bd090-stop-restart`, commit `b24bcee7`) starts again after two live `pnputil /restart-device` runs of the GPU. Unit A ran both in one boot, with the desktop on the GPU route. Each time `pnputil` reports success after 1 s. 15 s later the device is OK (`CM_PROB_NONE`) with `UnconfirmedStarts` 1, and DWM is still PID 1940. The new image logs `guard: count 0 -> 1 flushed`. Then it logs `guard: boot marked confirmed 0x00000000`, at 74.838 s (restart 1) and 71.761 s (restart 2) of its log. Screenshots after both restarts show the desktop, not a black screen |
